See `ailment-catalog-pattern.md`.\n\n## Operating Disciplines\n\nHow I work, especially at version ships and high-stakes lore edits:\n- **On VERSION bumps:** backfill `versioning-release-types.md` history, add the cache-clear footer if cache-affecting, bump all four version-bearing plugin manifests to `1.<VERSION>.0`, promote any newly-named principle to its own topic. (Full curation disciplines live in `role.md`.)\n- **Pre-ship review:** multi-lens review iterated until a round finds nothing worth fixing (convergence is the ship signal) — **run it via `/lr:trilens-loop`** rather than hand-assembling the fan-out; the skill enforces cold-context reviewer independence, the APPLIED/DECLINED ledger, the \"a silent round is not a clean round\" guard, and rail-removal disclosure, while lens *choice* and triage judgement stay mine. Brief reviewers with the **goal, not the rationale** — rationale pre-empts the criticism you're paying for. Two triage facts from measuring a live round: **~94% of a loop's tokens stay inside the subagents** (a reviewer's budget is its own reasoning, not the material handed to it — so withholding the diff is cost-neutral, and the exchange contract must be justified on independence and host-context preservation, never on token savings), and **convergent findings from two independent lenses are strong evidence, not redundancy** — reword rather than defend. Sonnet boot-as-self review remains the separate single-lens, role-as-perspective tool for high-stakes single edits. See `trilens-loop-feature.md`, `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`, `sonnet-subagent-review-pattern.md`. **Second, empirical leg (v18+):** for procedure docs covered by the lifecycle testing harness, also run the relevant scenarios against real engine execution before shipping — review catches reasoning issues, the harness catches model-execution-fidelity issues invisible to a strong-model reviewer — and the fidelity axis is **engine, not just model tier** (run scenarios on every engine, using the cheapest practical default tier unless explicitly overriding: Claude Code -> haiku, Codex -> gpt-5.4-mini, Cursor -> composer-2.5; the same model tier can behave differently by engine, and mid-procedure step insertions are the highest-risk for a silent skip). See `lifecycle-testing-harness.md`, `execution-testing-catches-blind-ambiguity.md`, `haiku-ambiguity-detector.md`.\n- **A gate result belongs to an artifact state** — a converged review loop and a green lifecycle run each certify only the tree they actually ran against. An edit landed after the gates pass is ungated: re-run the affected gate, or revert and file a follow-up. Never report \"converged and green\" for a tree neither gate saw, and record *which* state a result belongs to. Also: an environment failure mid-run (e.g. macOS TCC revocation) or the engine resolving a *different* plugin tree makes results **uninterpretable**, not red — fix and re-run rather than debugging the code under test. See `post-convergence-edits-need-their-own-gate.md`, `macos-documents-permission-loss-mid-session.md`.\n- **A gate cannot be a model self-report** — a gate must not be implemented in the medium it gates. Ask what evidence it rests on and whether the thing under test could have produced that evidence; if yes, it is a self-report wearing a gate's name. \"Both engines have coverage\" is not evidence parity. See `a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md`.\n- **A failure list is a hypothesis until someone reads the transcripts** — an assertion message names what was observed, never why. Re-triage from stored logs before planning fixes; stored artifacts plus `stat` routinely beat fresh engine probes on cost and certainty. Classify each text assertion as mid-run (needs the transcript) or end-state (final message suffices) — mixing them changes what a test means per engine. See `v31-lifecycle-rerun-partial-green-2026-07-27.md`, `transcript-vs-final-message-assertions.md`.\n- **Decide where the guardrail lives before writing the topic** — lore is retrieved when a task cues it, and a one-off command cues nothing; a trap recorded only as knowledge protects nobody, including its author. Name the point-of-use site (script check, exact command in the doc, test) as part of the fix, and prefer a deterministic harness check over a human prep step. See `point-of-use-guardrails-beat-recorded-lore.md`, `docs-engines-convention.md` § Engine traps belong in the binding.\n- **Verify before asserting** — check filesystem/state directly before \"fixing\" a suspected bug; verify *which* bug, not just whether. **Pointed inward:** before declaring a known finding moot — especially a ship-blocking one — read the lore rule it rests on rather than reconstructing it; reconstruction keeps a rule's motivating case and drops its actual obligation, and those diverge precisely when dismissal feels justified (`check-own-lore-before-dismissing-a-finding.md`). Same reflex, two more sites: fetch volatile external facts (prices, model IDs, rate limits) live with a dated citation rather than trusting memory — \"couldn't verify\" licenses marking a value unavailable, not guessing; and after any scoped/read-only subagent or fork returns, verify its actual filesystem footprint (`git status`, `git worktree list`) rather than trusting its summary — a capable fork acts on the largest goal it can see in inherited context unless scoped *against* it explicitly. See `verify-before-acting-on-suspected-bugs.md`, `fetch-volatile-facts-live-not-memory.md`, `fork-scope-creep-under-standing-goal.md`.\n- **Curation meta-rules:** name foundational principles as their own topics; single canonical source (pointer, don't restate — and its design-time cousin: reuse an existing correlation/identity signal before inventing new plumbing); don't defer completable bounded sweeps; graduated verification (confidence, not boolean). See `naming-foundational-principles.md`, `single-canonical-source-discipline.md`, `reuse-existing-correlation-signal.md`, `feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md`, `graduated-verification-confidence.md`.\n- **User-feedback working style:** ranked-shortlist over exhaustive enumeration; confirm before writing durable lore mid-session; in design dialogues, write the draft only when the user triggers it (decisions are safe in conversation — don't repeatedly move to persist); populate dry-run counters with would-be outcomes; \"enforce X\" ≠ add a required schema field; for broad/emotionally-loaded open-ended asks, decompose into hidden axes and sequence a build order by dependency (cheapest/highest-leverage first, flashiest/most-structural last) rather than proposing a menu or jumping to implementation; on a second round of pushback on the same axis (length, tone, scope), act on the next ask instead of re-justifying — a second \"no\" is not a request for more reasoning; a **short measurement question gets a short factual answer**, with the interesting generalisation offered rather than delivered, and **several style skills invoked at once is a stop signal**, not a preference tweak (hold the style for the rest of the session). See `feedback-too-many-words.md`, `feedback-confirm-before-writing-lore.md`, `feedback-draft-only-when-user-triggers.md`, `feedback-schemas-as-enforcement-overreach.md`, `feedback-layered-decomposition-for-open-ended-asks.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`, `feedback-comply-promptly-after-repeated-pushback.md`.\n\n## Key Constraints\n\n- `lore-context.md` ≤ 50K tokens; **shape over size** — working-knowledge + summary-topic references, not an index (see `lore-context-shape-discipline.md`).\n- Lore topics: atomic, <5K tokens preferred, plain markdown, no frontmatter.\n- Descriptor frontmatter: `lore-repo.md` = `description` + `version` (+ optional `repos:`); `role.md` = `description` only.\n- Command filenames: lowercase/digits/hyphens, ≤64 chars.\n- Placeholders: `<workspace>`, `<lore-agent-repo>`, `<guest-lore-agent-repo>`, `<agent-name>`, `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`.\n- **CWD safety:** never `cd` when later tools depend on cwd — use `git -C <repo>`. **Portable shell:** assume BSD/macOS, no GNU-only binaries (`timeout`); bound commands via the Bash-tool timeout.\n- See `conventions.md`, `placeholder-vocabulary.md`, `tooling-cwd-safety.md`, `portable-shell-in-framework-docs.md`.\n\n## Onboarding-Doc Authoring\n\nCo-authoring framework onboarding docs for adopting teams is part of the role. Two distinct genres now exist: **`onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md`** (long-form prose pitching a human reader) and **`paste-link-installer-doc-genre.md`** (a doc written *to the AI agent* as the literal installer, meant to be pasted as a link — shipped as `QUICKSTART.md` + per-engine `INSTALL-<ENGINE>.md`). Load the identity-layer framings first, then the toolkit: the two genre topics above, `use-cases-via-parallel-consult-pattern.md`, `terminology-domain-collision-trap.md`, `agent-as-universal-working-environment.md`, `in-flight-skill-teaching-pattern.md`. Pre-ship review for either genre uses `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`'s multi-lens fan-out; the installer genre additionally needs the **AI-installer (literal executor)** lens (`ai-installer-review-lens.md`) — it catches execution-fidelity bugs (e.g. `skill-doc-filename-divergence-bug-class.md`) the newcomer/editorial lenses miss. Landing-page placement of a self-referential/meta example differs from long-narrative placement — primacy goes to the strongest CTA; see `onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md` § placement note. A recurring funnel bug: an author writing from the fresh-start perspective railroads readers into create-your-first-agent and leaves the **team-join path** invisible at every layer (README prose, QUICKSTART, and the INSTALL AI-agent preambles) — check all layers, and keep the fork question verbatim-identical across sites (`onboarding-funnel-team-join-path.md`). Adopter-facing prose carries the product name **\"Lore Agents\"** while the engine keeps `lore-framework`/`lr` (`lore-agents-product-name.md`). First instance: the Activities team's intro doc.\n\n## Active Design Explorations\n\n- **lr-dev / Dark Factory (DF)** — major direction; a `lr` module for SDLC automation toward an autonomous \"dark factory\" SDLC. Per-repo artifacts + narrative context live in a `<repo>-df` backbone (a `repo-lore/<file>/` mirror: `file-lore.md` narrative landing + flat structured aspect subdirs like `ula/`). Skills not agents; persistence external. First aspect: **AIQA/ULA** (`/lr:df-repo-init`, `/lr:df-ula-file`) — unit-level analysis with a bug-verification track, BETA. The DF/ULA design thread is closed and the module ships as BETA. Anchor: `lr-dev-direction.md`; see `df-per-repo-backbone.md`, `aiqa-ula-feature.md`, `df-module-conventions.md`, `workflow-primitive-operational-notes.md`.\n- **Autonomous agents / Lore Beings** — agents as always-on background collaborators with persistent task state, raising for input only when needed. Concrete steps taken: `/lr:spawn-teammate` (multi-agent substrate, v10) and the v18 **`lr-wait`** primitive — the first *inbound-signal* step: an agent blocks on an event and an external actor (cron/CI/webhook/human, via `lr-emit`) wakes it with text. **The beings design is settled (2026-07-19): the module is _Lore Beings_.** A being is an ordinary lore agent plus a `being.md` descriptor; the **Being Keeper** (`lrb`) is deterministic substrate (never an LLM). **MVP is CLI-only**; engines are explicit user config. Budget = daily-USD spawn gate + per-task wall-clock kill. **Engine kinds: `claude`, `codex`, and `cursor`** — cursor landed in framework v28: requires `--plugin-dir` at `engines add`, claude-shaped JSON result + flat-cost fallback. **Keeper-specific real-engine lifecycle coverage now exists** (`tests/lifecycle/keeper_harness.py` + `test_lrb_lifecycle.py`, 10 scenarios after the 2026-07-20 fifth pass added B2/B3, separate higher-blast-radius gate `LR_LIFECYCLE_KEEPER=1`, verified claude 6/6 + codex 1/1 + cursor 1/1 at the recommended-minimum tier). That fifth pass found and fixed a real production bug, not just a coverage gap: `cursor-agent`'s sandboxed shell tool escapes `_kill`'s `killpg` by running spawned commands in a freshly `setsid`'d session, which left a real orphaned process on the test machine before the fix — `_kill` now also walks the full ppid-descendant tree and signals every descendant directly, enumerated *before* any ancestor is signaled (killing the ancestor first risks the OS reparenting a survivor to PID 1 and erasing the ppid link). **The Keeper substrate shipped BETA in v28, and v29 added the in-engine command surface `/lr:being`** — one skill with subcommands (status/init/create/validate/logs/keeper/engine/workspace/pause/resume) over the same deterministic `lrb` CLI, rather than a `lrb-*` skill per operation. Ship record: v28 at framework commit `5e00209`, tag `lr--v1.28.0`; the standard lifecycle gate passed by persisted broad matrix plus targeted reruns. Real-engine verification sharpened two per-kind contract gaps (both backlog schema decisions, not silently patched): the `cursor` kind is empirically cost-blind (no `total_cost_usd`), so its flat `--session-cost-usd` fallback is load-bearing not optional; the `claude` kind has no `--plugin-dir` field, so a claude-kind being needs a wrapper-script `command` to load `lr:` skills at all. Two further findings from that same review pass — budget-enforcement edge cases and an unattended-full-permission trust gap — were deliberately deferred to the backlog rather than fixed. Chronicler week-long soak and persistent `--launchd` install still user-triggered. Open gap: headless permissions (`permission_mode: full` vs future scoped-tools), and self-scheduling under the safe default. Anchor: `lore-beings-design.md`; see `cursor-agent-real-invocation-contract.md`, `engine-kinds-design-decision.md`, `lifecycle-testing-harness.md` § Keeper coverage, `keeper-spawn-prompt-boilerplate-distraction.md`, `lore-beings-mvp-takeover-review.md`, `kill-tree-enumerate-before-signal-ordering.md`, `codex-exec-real-invocation-contract.md`, `macos-ps-o-multi-field-single-line.md`, `agent-being-consciousness-substrate-split.md`, `unenforceable-caps-are-prompt-theater.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`, `autonomous-agents-vision.md`, `wait-primitive-feature.md`, `framework-improvements-backlog.md` § Major Directions § Autonomous Agents / Lore Beings.\n- **Multi-engine portability (Codex, Cursor)** — **the ports are shipped, not in flight.** Claude\n  Code, Codex, and Cursor are all Tier-1 supported, so a mixed-engine team shares one agent repo.\n  Claude Code remains the reference path: shared procedure docs are written in Claude terms, and other\n  engines override only at binding points. What made this tractable: the knowledge substrate (agent\n  repos, `lore-repo.md`, `role.md`, `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, git) was already engine-agnostic, so\n  the port was **packaging, not redesign** — the whole surface is **5 adapter bindings** via the\n  `docs/engines/` convention (framework-root, invocation-syntax, subagent-spawn, memory-file,\n  runtime-bounding) plus Boot Step-0 engine selection. Both engines have native in-session subagents\n  (Codex `spawn_agent`/`wait_agent`; Cursor `Task`), so the feared Tier-B nucleus is proven, and one\n  repo carries both skill namespaces (canonical `skills/<skill>/` for Claude, `.cursor-skills/lr-<skill>/`\n  wrappers for Cursor, kept in sync by `scripts/sync-cursor-skills` and `/lr:check` #21).\n\n  Standing operational facts worth carrying: **trust rollout/tool-call logs, not model self-report**,\n  when validating an engine path; Codex's default sandbox blocks `.git` writes and network, so the\n  supported finalization path needs `.git` writable through launch/config (a commit-blocked run is\n  degraded fallback, not a merge failure); Codex per-agent shortcut register/unregister/list remains an\n  unvalidated implementation gap. The **\"framework is prose executed by the model\"** risk is\n  empirically retired for the exercised paths, and the substrate half has quantitative backing — the\n  quality benchmark showed positive lore-utilization uplift on every engine+model config, with the\n  nuance that **model–engine fit beats model tier**. Cross-engine support is a *supporting* fact in\n  positioning, not the headline (the 2026-07-20 landscape re-survey found competitors claiming it too);\n  lead with the triad. Anchor: `multi-engine-portability-direction.md`; per-engine entry points\n  `claude-engine-capabilities.md`, `codex-engine-capabilities.md`, `cursor-engine-capabilities.md`;\n  see also `docs-engines-convention.md`, `subagent-as-optimization-vs-subagent-as-semantics.md`,\n  `claude-coupling-inventory-and-port-tiers.md`, `cursor-dual-skill-tree-one-repo.md`,\n  `quality-benchmark-feature.md`, `positioning-triad-differentiation.md`, `port-landing-next-steps.md`.\n- **Lore housekeeping / consolidation \"sleep\" pass** and the **simplification/subtraction** review item — active follow-ups from the 2026-06-13 architecture review; see `framework-improvements-backlog.md`. That review's settled dispositions (incl. DF-inside-`lr` and team-shared/multi-author as deliberate, not defects — don't re-raise) live in `architecture-review-dispositions.md`. A newer 2026-07-02 review added two further backlog items (post-merge diff verification, recall-time staleness surfacing) — see `framework-improvements-backlog.md` § Merge Quality, § Search / Scaling.\n- Parked: workdir-as-reference-library; vector-DB search (until >100 topics/agent); the session-as-durable-artifact cluster (boot auto-push, boot-context cache, suspend/resume, JSONL archive). All in `framework-improvements-backlog.md`.\n- **v25 workspace layer (pull + init)** — implemented locally in `lore-framework` commit `0311ab6`.\n  Hard renames: workspace-sync→workspace-pull, init→workspace-init. Two-level repo declarations\n  (`lore-workspace.md` + domain `repos:`), optional workspace-as-git-repo envelope, cursor wrapper\n  regeneration, checks #22–23. Full lifecycle remains the pre-push gate. See\n  `v25-workspace-pull-init-design.md`, `workspace-meta-repo-pattern.md`.\n\n## Current State\n\nWorkspace holds three canonical repos: **`lore-framework/`** (plugin), **`lore-framework-dev/`**\n(this repo — lore-architect lore, tests, drafts), and **`lore-agents/`** (personal agents).\n\n`lore-framework/` is **clean and pushed at v30** on `main`. The three newest *shipped* capability\nsurfaces are all live: **Lore Beings** (Being Keeper substrate + `/lr:being` command surface, BETA),\n**Markdown session archives** under `agents/<agent>/archive/YYYY/MM/`, and **`/lr:trilens-loop`**\n(iterated three-lens review). Three engines are Tier-1 supported — Claude Code, Codex, Cursor.\n\n**v31 `lr-core` is built and reviewed; agent-repo side is on `lore-framework-dev` `main`, plugin\nstill PARKED.** Deterministic substrate + literate Script Fallback Contract + reworked\n`docs/trilens-loop.md` live on `lore-framework` branch `wip/lr-core-v31` @ `b824da5` (worktree\n`.worktrees/lore-framework/lr-core-v31/`) — **not merged, not a ship**.\n\nLifecycle standing: **one confirmed v31 defect** (Codex `test_07`, fixed on `b824da5` —\n`script-emits-data-doc-owns-the-words.md`), **one undetermined** (Codex `test_08`, undecidable\nuntil transcript capture landed), and **the whole Cursor shard uninterpretable** — it ran against\na v30 plugin. Check\n`v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md` before related work, and read\n`v31-lifecycle-rerun-partial-green-2026-07-27.md`'s corrected triage rather than any remembered\nfailure list. See also `lifecycle-harness-plugin-identity-unverified.md`,\n`a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md`, `transcript-vs-final-message-assertions.md`,\n`cursor-cloud-plugin-rehydrates-over-plugin-dir.md`, `trilens-loop-v31-restructured.md`,\n`literate-accelerator-pattern.md`.\n\nBoth pre-ship gates are in working order and both are routinely run: the review loop via\n`/lr:trilens-loop`, and the real-engine lifecycle suite via `tests/lifecycle/` (plus the separate\nhigher-blast-radius `LR_LIFECYCLE_KEEPER=1` Keeper track and the `LR_QUALITY=1` benchmark track).\nSee `trilens-loop-feature.md`, `lifecycle-testing-harness.md`, `lore-beings-design.md`,\n`session-summaries-feature.md`.\n\n## Running Backlog & Standing Improvement List\n\n`framework-improvements-backlog.md` is the canonical list of deferred items; its § Ship Closures\narchives per-ship gate dispositions.     "type": "input_text",
    "text": "# Searching Your Lore\n\nRead this when you need to find or recall topics from your lore. Covers both the agent-initiated case (you decide you need lore) and the user-triggered case (`/lr:recall`). Also covers the multi-agent case when guests are attached to the host session.\n\nDefault scope is `agents/<agent>/lore/` only. Do not include `sessions/`, `archive/`, or other agent subdirectories in ordinary lore recall. Search/read `archive/` only when the user explicitly asks for full session logs or a particular session, or when the task specifically requires session-log evidence.\n\n## Preferred Mechanism: Subagent Scan\n\nFor any non-trivial search, **dispatch an `Explore` subagent** with a structured search brief. The subagent reads relevant lore topics directly, synthesizes findings, and returns a compact result. This keeps your main context clean and uses the LLM's own semantic understanding — stronger than keyword matching for finding topics by *meaning* rather than by *exact term*.\n\n> **Engine note.** \"`Explore` subagent\" and \"single-message parallel dispatch\" describe Claude Code. If your engine profile (`<framework-root>/docs/engines/<engine>.md`) defines a subagent-spawn override, use its read-only spawn instead — e.g. on Codex, `spawn_agent` with role `explorer`, gathered via `wait_agent`.\n\nWhy a subagent:\n- Reading 10–100 topic files in your main context wastes tokens you need for the user's task\n- The synthesis can be inlined cheaply (a few hundred words)\n- `Explore` is fast and tool-restricted (Read/Grep/Glob, no edit/agent tools) — exactly what's needed for a search\n\n### Give the subagent a topic manifest\n\nBefore dispatching, generate the reading map:\n\n```\npython3 \"<framework-root>/scripts/lr-core\" scan --agent-dir \"<agent-dir>\"\n```\n\nIt returns every topic with its title line, last-commit date, age in days, and a staleness flag — one git call for the whole directory, rather than the subagent globbing and sampling files to work out what exists. Pass the output to the subagent as part of its brief (write it to a temp file and give the path if it is large; a 150-topic manifest is sizeable, and it belongs in the *subagent's* context, not yours).\n\nTwo things this buys beyond speed:\n\n- **Search by title, not just by filename.** The manifest exposes what each topic actually claims, so the subagent picks what to read on meaning rather than guessing from the name.\n- **Age-awareness.** Lore accumulates for years; a topic last touched 14 months ago may describe a superseded design. Tell the subagent to **note the age of anything it surfaces, and flag findings from `stale: true` topics as needing verification** before they are acted on. Age is a caution flag, not a disqualifier — plenty of old topics are still exactly right.\n\nIf the script fails to complete, apply the **Script Fallback Contract** (`<framework-root>/docs/conventions.md`). Unlike the other call sites, there's no hand-executed equivalent worth running here (read `cmd_scan`'s docstring (the numbered steps; `scan_lore` below it is the implementation) in `<framework-root>/scripts/lr-core` if you want the exact manifest logic) — simply dispatch the subagent without a manifest; it will list and read the directory itself, which is the historical behavior and still works.\n\n## Active Agents: Fan Out When Guests Are Attached\n\nIf guests are attached to the host session (via `/lr:attach`), the search runs across **all active agents' lore directories** — host + every attached guest. Execute this by dispatching **one `Explore` subagent per active agent in parallel** (all subagent calls in a single message), each scoped to one agent's `lore/` directory.\n\nEach subagent receives the same brief but is told which agent's lore to scan. Each returns its own compact synthesis. The host merges the results for the user, grouped by agent so it's clear whose lore each finding came from.\n\nWhen there are no guests, the fan-out reduces to a single subagent — behavior is identical to the single-agent case. You don't need to branch your procedure; just enumerate the active agents (host plus any attached guests) and dispatch N subagents.\n\n**Why parallel, not serial:** each subagent runs independently and there are no dependencies between them. Single-message parallel dispatch is both faster and the standard pattern for independent subagent work.\n\n## How to Prepare a Search Brief\n\nThe quality of what comes back depends entirely on the brief. A good brief has four parts:\n\n### 1. The Task — One or Two Sentences\n\nWhat you're actively working on. Be concrete:\n\n- Good: *\"Refactoring the framework update process to support a release-notes track alongside migrations.\"*\n- Bad: *\"Working on framework stuff.\"*\n\n### 2. Session Context That Shapes Relevance\n\nTwo to five bullets covering decisions made, constraints surfaced, paths or filenames being touched, open questions. This is what lets the subagent distinguish *relevant* from *generally related*.\n\n### 3. The Angle — If You Have One\n\nIf the user provided a hint (`/lr:recall anything about versioning`), center the brief on it.\n\nIf not, describe the *kind* of lore that would help — prior decisions, operational lessons, domain facts, known pitfalls, past trade-offs. This anchors the subagent's reading.\n\n### 4. The Output Shape You Want Back\n\nAsk explicitly for:\n- A compact synthesis (under ~400 words) grouped by theme\n- Each finding tagged with its source topic filename\n- Explicit call-outs for topics worth reading in full yourself\n- *\"Nothing relevant found\"* if that's the honest answer — don't invent relevance\n\nKeep the whole brief under ~300 words. The subagent has access to your full `lore/` directory; you don't need to summarize what's *in* lore, only what's in *this session*.\n\n## Worked Example\n\n**Brief sent to the subagent:**\n\n> Search the lore at `agents/lore-architect/lore/` for topics relevant to my current task.\n>\n> **Task:** Designing a `/lr:recall` skill that lets the user explicitly trigger a lore search mid-session. The synthesis is inlined into the main agent's working context.\n>\n> **Session context:**\n> - Decided against introducing a vector DB at current scale — using LLM-driven subagent scan instead\n> - Framework bumping to v3 with new search guidance and a recall skill\n> - Keeping `agent-boot.md` compact — detailed search guidance lives in a separate doc\n> - Following the existing skill/doc separation pattern\n>\n> **Looking for:** prior decisions on user-invocable skills, transparency patterns (showing intermediate results to the user), past discussion of subagent usage in the framework.\n>\n> **Output:** compact synthesis grouped by theme, ≤400 words, with topic filenames. Flag anything I should read in full. Say \"nothing relevant\" if true.\n\n**Expected response shape:**\n\n> **User-invocable skill conventions** (`slash-command-system.md`): all user-invocable skills use the `/lr:` prefix; generated artifacts use `/lr-`. Skill files are thin pointers; logic lives in `docs/`.\n>\n> **Skill/doc separation** (`skill-doc-pattern.md`): every skill is one line pointing at `<framework-root>/docs/<name>.md`. Recall should follow this exactly.\n>\n> **No prior recall pattern** — no existing topic discusses user-triggered context enrichment. New ground.\n>\n> **Read in full:** `slash-command-system.md` for the conventions you'll be following.\n\n## What to Do With the Result\n\n1. **Show the synthesis to the user.** Transparency — they should see what got pulled in and judge whether the recall was useful. If multiple active agents were scanned (fan-out), group results by agent so it's clear whose lore each finding came from.\n2. **Carry it as working context** for the rest of the session.\n3. **Read flagged topics in full yourself** — don't trust synthesis alone for load-bearing details.\n\n## Direct-Tool Fallback\n\nWhen you know exactly what you're looking for or just need a quick check, the standard tools are fine — no subagent needed:\n\n- **Glob / list** — see what topics exist\n- **Grep** — search for a specific keyword across topics\n- **Read** — read a specific topic in full\n- **`git log`** — when a topic was created (`git -C <lore-agent-repo> log --diff-filter=A -- agents/<agent-name>/lore/<topic>.md`), last updated (`git -C <lore-agent-repo> log -1 -- agents/<agent-name>/lore/<topic>.md`), or full history (`git -C <lore-agent-repo> log --follow -- agents/<agent-name>/lore/<topic>.md`). Use `git -C` rather than `cd`ing into the repo — the shell CWD is shared with Glob and Grep.\n- **Deleted topics** remain in git history — searchable if you need past knowledge that was later removed\n\nRule of thumb: use the subagent when you're searching by **meaning**; use direct tools when you're searching by **name** or **exact Verified inconsistencies — fix now (bounded, \"v28 hygiene ship\" tier)\n\n### A0. `lr-core` CLI — script-back the mechanical halves of high-frequency skills — DESIGN DRAFTED\nDesign + phased execution plan in `workdir/draft-lr-core-cli.md` (2026-07-25 design dialogue;\nimplementation deferred by the user — resume from the draft). Reference rot + `/lr:check` mechanical-check reliability — OPEN\nDeterministic sweep (2026-07-18) flagged 14 unresolved topic-style references in\nlore-architect's 147-topic graph; ≥8 are genuine rot from renames/deletions:\n`contributions-feature.md` (referenced by 7 topics), `workspace-sync.md` (v25 rename),\n`dev-repo-lore.md`, `dev-module-conventions.md` (DF-rename leftovers),\n`codex-binding-design.md`, `beta-refinement-workflow.md`,\n`codex-multiagent-research.md`, `codex-multiagent-live-capture.md`. Overridden, disclosed, and generalized as its own topic — see\n`diverged-branch-diff-misread-as-merge-outcome.md`.\n\n**Outstanding for v31 as of the 2026-07-28 re-triage** (supersedes all older resume lists, and\nin particular the six-item failure list this section used to carry):\n\n1. **Re-run Codex `test_07` and `test_08`.** `test_07`'s real defect is fixed on `b824da5`\n   (`script-emits-data-doc-owns-the-words.md`); `test_08` was never decidable on Codex until\n   `RunResult.transcript` landed (`transcript-vs-final-message-assertions.md`).\n2. **Re-run the entire Cursor shard** with `check_cursor_plugin_sources()` in force. Its\n   2026-07-27 numbers carry no information — the suite ran against a v30 plugin.\n3. **Gate this session's four ungated commits** (see the ledger above): a trilens round covering\n   `b824da5`'s boot-path doc change, plus a lifecycle run that actually sees them.\n4. Version-ship discipline once the gate is genuinely green: four manifests to `1.31.0`, the\n   `versioning-release-types.md` v31 entry, the cache-clear footer (cache-affecting), then merge\n   **`lore-framework`** `wip/lr-core-v31` → `main` and push. (`lore-framework-dev` already on main,\n   including A7.)\n5. Fold `trilens-loop-v31-restructured.md` into `trilens-loop-feature.md` when the plugin doc ships.\n6. Restore the Cursor cloud-plugin backup (`~/.cursor/plugins-backup-v31-*`) after parked-branch\n   testing if normal IDE installs should return — and note that the move-aside must be **re-checked\n   after the fact**, not trusted (`cursor-cloud-plugin-rehydrates-over-plugin-dir.md`).\n\nPlugin branch tip is now `b824da5` on `wip/lr-core-v31`, clean, not merged/pushed.\n\n**Not a v31 item:** the `agent-boot.md` size regression measured 2026-07-28\n(`agent-boot-doc-grew-when-scripted.md`) is deliberately deferred to v32 — reopening the\nmost-read procedure doc now moves the ship further out. Queued as A8 on\n`workdir/what-to-improve.md`.\n\n## Where it lives\n\nBranch `wip/lr-core-v31` in **`lore-framework`** (still the only unmerged side), checked out at\n`<workspace>/.worktrees/lore-framework/lr-core-v31/`. **`lore-framework-dev` main now includes the\nformer WIP** (merged 2026-07-27); its worktree may still exist at\n`.worktrees/lore-framework-dev/lr-core-v31/` but is no longer the source of truth for agent-repo\ncontent. Top-level `lore-framework` `main` remains clean at v30 — nothing of the plugin work is\nvisible from a normal boot on main. `docs/trilens-loop.md` on main is still the full 325-line\nversion described in `trilens-loop-feature.md` — see `trilens-loop-v31-restructured.md` for the\nparked plugin branch's version of that doc, and `trilens-loop-deliberately-minimal-2026-07-25.md`\nfor the standing don't-auto-restore rule.\n\n## Resuming\n\nDon't reconstruct the plan from scratch. **A7 (both holes now closed), the Codex repoint, and a\nCodex lifecycle shard are done.** Older resume bullets about \"never ran lifecycle\" / \"repoint\nsources\" are stale — and so is any six-item failure list: read\n`v31-lifecycle-rerun-partial-green-2026-07-27.md`'s **corrected** triage, not a remembered\nversion of it.\n\nNext, in order:\n1. Re-run Codex `test_07` / `test_08` and the full Cursor shard (see Outstanding above).\n2. Gate the four ungated commits from 2026-07-28.\n3. Ship the plugin when the gate is genuinely green (manifests, version-history, cache-clear,\n   merge `wip/lr-core-v31` → main).\n4. Collapse `trilens-loop-v31-restructured.md` into `trilens-loop-feature.md` on ship; restore\n   the Cursor cloud-plugin backup if normal installs should return.\n\nBefore starting *any* fresh work on `lr-core`, the Script Fallback Contract, or `trilens-loop.md`, check\nfor this branch/worktree pair first — a second attempt at the same design without checking would\nduplicate work already done and reviewed.\n\n## Relation to the normal parking drill\n\nThis is a different flavor of parking than `parked-design-preservation-pattern.md`'s revert-and-\nworkdir-draft drill: that pattern is for early/mid-draft design work reverted back to committed main\nplus a `workdir/draft-*.md`. Here the work is *complete and reviewed* — it stays on its own\nbranch/worktree rather than being reverted, because reverting fully-implemented, twice-reviewed code\nwould throw away real work for no reason. Use the revert-and-draft drill for early-stage design pivots;\nuse branch/worktree parking (this instance) for complete-but-not-yet-shipped work that the user wants\nheld rather than pushed through or discarded.\n\n## See Also\n\n- `trilens-loop-v31-restructured.md` — what `docs/trilens-loop.md` looks like on this branch now.\n- `trilens-loop-deliberately-minimal-2026-07-25.md` — the standing don't-auto-restore rule for that\n  doc, and the record of the original compression.\n- `check-own-lore-before-dismissing-a-finding.md` — the near-miss that would have dropped the\n  `cursor.md` item unresolved.\n- `parked-design-preservation-pattern.md` — the sibling parking pattern for early-draft (not complete)\n  work.\n- `worktrees-convention.md` — the underlying worktree mechanism.\n- `feedback-comply-promptly-after-repeated-pushback.md` — the working-style lesson from the same rough\n  session.\n- `post-convergence-edits-need-their-own-gate.md` — the framing behind why the addendum's surface\n  needed its own trilens pass rather than inheriting round 2's clearance — satisfied by the round-1\n  review above.\n- `git-dash-c-needs-toplevel-guard.md` — the mutating-git-operation bug found and fixed in round 1\n  (nested-repo `git -C` escape).\n- `verify-regression-tests-via-mutation.md` — how the two round-2 regression tests added in round 1\n  were verified to actually pin their bugs.\n- `literate-accelerator-pattern.md` — the pattern this work introduced. It **is** in this repo's\n  lore now (landed on `main` via `8fc46f0`); the 2026-07-26 addendum's \"worktree only\" note is\n  historical.\n- `script-emits-data-doc-owns-the-words.md` — the accelerator constraint v31's own lifecycle run\n  taught: the script emits data, the doc owns user-facing words.\n- `a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md`, `transcript-vs-final-message-assertions.md` — the two\n  harness defects that made the 2026-07-27 failure list untrustworthy.\n- `agent-boot-doc-grew-when-scripted.md` — the v32-deferred size regression in the doc this\n  release scripted.\n- `lifecycle-harness-plugin-identity-unverified.md` — the 2026-07-27 finding that Codex/Cursor silently\n  resolved an installed v30 plugin instead of the worktree under test, invalidating both engines'\n  results from this session's lifecycle run.\n- `macos-var-symlink-realpath-ambiguity.md` — the macOS `pwd`-vs-`realpath` bug behind `test_boot`'s\n  flaky scenario, found and fixed during the same run.\n- `flaky-scenario-diagnosis-needs-ab-baseline.md` — the methodology correction (A/B against the v30\n  baseline) that distinguished \"pre-existing flaky scenario\" from \"v31 regression\" for that same bug.\n- `diverged-branch-diff-misread-as-merge-outcome.md` — the overridden `BLOCKER` from this session's\n  trilens round, misreading `main`'s divergence from this branch as a preview of merge behavior.\n# v31 Lifecycle Re-run After A7 — Re-Triaged (run 2026-07-27, triage corrected 2026-07-28)\n\nFirst Codex/Cursor lifecycle against `lore-framework` `wip/lr-core-v31` @ `cd8ece1` after the A7\nplugin-identity gate landed and engines were repointed (Codex marketplace → worktree; Cursor cloud\nplugin moved aside — see `cursor-cloud-plugin-rehydrates-over-plugin-dir.md`).\n\n> **Read this section first.** The run's original triage listed six model-fidelity failures. On\n> 2026-07-28 the stored logs were read line by line and **four of the six were not what they looked\n> like** — three were one environment bug and one was an undecidable assertion. Acting on the old\n> list would have sent a session writing doc fixes for a harness/environment problem.\n\n| Engine | Result | Standing |\n|---|---|---|\n| Codex | 6/7 modules | **Valid.** One genuine defect, one undetermined. |\n| Cursor | nominally 4/7 | **Uninterpretable, not 4/7.** Ran against a v30 plugin. |\n\nResult dirs: `tests/lifecycle/results/20260727T140740Z-standard/` (Codex),\n`.../20260727T140949Z-standard/` (Cursor). Ground truth for the re-triage:\n`results/20260727T140949Z-standard/logs/`.\n\n## Corrected triage\n\n**Genuine v31 defect — exactly one:**\n\n- **Codex `test_07`** (repo-ahead skew) — `lr-core preflight` emitted a warning string that read\n  like a finished message, so the model printed it instead of routing into `version-check.md`'s\n  engine-specific remedy. Root cause and fix in\n  `script-emits-data-doc-owns-the-words.md`; applied on branch `b824da5`.\n\n**Undetermined, not failing:**\n\n- **Codex `test_08`** (Script Fallback notify) — the notice is emitted mid-boot, but the Codex\n  driver captured only `--output-last-message`, so a compliant run and a violating run are\n  indistinguishable in the artifacts. Harness defect. Fixed by `RunResult.transcript` (`467b009`);\n  the scenario must be re-run to get a verdict. See\n  `transcript-vs-final-message-assertions.md`.\n\n**Not fidelity failures at all — one environment bug, seen three times (Cursor):**\n\n- `test_05` — `AssertionError: '30' != '31'`. The repo *was* stamped, to 30. The model executed the\n  upgrade correctly against a v30 plugin.\n- `test_08` — reply: *\"the repo version (31) differs from the framework (30)\"*, followed by a normal\n  fast-path boot. The deliberately broken `lr-core` copy was never loaded, so no fallback triggered\n  and there was nothing to notify about.\n- `test_21` (update dry-run) — reports framework 30. Same cause.\n\nCursor's shard ran against an installed/cached v30 plugin despite the pre-run move-aside, because\nthe cloud plugin rehydrated 23 seconds after the move (`cursor-cloud-plugin-rehydrates-over-plugin-dir.md`)\nand A7's Cursor arm was a model self-report that could not see it\n(`a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md`). **Cursor has produced zero trustworthy v31 boot data.**\n\n**Harness overhead, not a scenario failure:**\n\n- `test_takeover` — 420s timeout. `run_matrix` probed once per engine, then every module subprocess\n  re-probed. Fixed by inheriting the verdict via an `engine|realpath|VERSION` token while still\n  running the deterministic filesystem check in each child.\n\n## Ship gate as it now stands\n\n1. Land the `test_07` fix (done on `b824da5`) and **re-run Codex `test_07` + `test_08`** — the\n   latter now has a transcript to assert against.\n2. **Re-run the whole Cursor shard** with the deterministic `check_cursor_plugin_sources()` gate in\n   force. Its previous numbers carry no information.\n3. Only then judge the gate green. Note that `b824da5` and the three dev-repo commits below are\n   themselves ungated — see `v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md`.\n\n## Standing lesson\n\n**A failure list is a hypothesis until someone reads the transcripts.** Six assertion messages\nproduced six plausible stories; four were wrong, and the wrong ones were the *confident-sounding*\nones (\"model didn't stamp the file\", \"model didn't notify\"). An assertion message names what was\nobserved, never why. Re-triage from stored logs before planning fixes — it cost zero engine calls\nhere (`cursor-cloud-plugin-rehydrate-timing` evidence came from `stat` plus stored stderr).\n\n## See Also\n\n- `v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md` — parking record, resume list, ungated-commit ledger.\n- `lifecycle-harness-plugin-identity-unverified.md` — the A7 gate, its two holes, and their fixes.\n- `a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md` — why Cursor's A7 arm passed while wrong.\n- `cursor-cloud-plugin-rehydrates-over-plugin-dir.md` — the rehydration behavior and its timing.\n- `script-emits-data-doc-owns-the-words.md` — the one confirmed defect.\n- `transcript-vs-final-message-assertions.md` — why `test_08` was undecidable.\n- `verify-before-acting-on-suspected-bugs.md` — the general reflex this run re-taught.\n# Lifecycle Harness Doesn't Verify Which Plugin Actually Loaded\n\n`tests/lifecycle/harness.py`'s `run_engine()` passes `--plugin-dir <framework_dir>` to every engine,\nbut on Codex and Cursor an **installed** plugin can silently win over that flag — the harness never\nverifies which plugin actually loaded before trusting the run's result. Found 2026-07-27, running the\nlifecycle suite against the parked `wip/lr-core-v31` branch for the first time.\n\n## Confirmed mechanism\n\nOn the machine this was found on: Codex's `~/.codex/config.toml` had\n`[marketplaces.lore-framework] source = \".../agent-workspace/lore-framework\"` (the **main** checkout,\nVERSION 30) and `[plugins.\"lr@lore-framework\"] enabled = true` — that installed plugin took precedence\nover the `--plugin-dir` flag pointing at the v31 worktree. Cursor's\n`~/.cursor/plugins/cache/zroslaw-lore-framework/.../11ec0df.../` was a cached plugin at commit `11ec0df`\n= tag `lr--v1.30.0`. Both engines ran a full lifecycle suite against **v30** while the harness, its\nlogs, and its summary all reported results as if v31 had been tested — nothing failed loudly; the tree\nunder test was just silently wrong.\n\nClaude Code was unaffected only because nothing is installed for it globally on this machine —\n`--plugin-dir` was the sole source. That's incidental to this machine's state, not a property of the\nharness or of Claude Code generally; a machine with a globally-installed Claude plugin could hit the\nsame failure mode there too.\n\n## Why this matters more than an ordinary coverage gap\n\nA green run under this condition is a **false green on the actual release artifact**, and a red run is\nequally uninterpretable — both look like valid gate results with nothing to distinguish them from a\ngenuine pass/fail. This is functionally the same danger as the sandboxed-review blind spot already in\nlore (`lore-beings-mvp-takeover-review.md`, and the general rule in `role.md` § Lore-Curation\nDisciplines), but via a **different mechanism**: not a blocked capability forcing an\n\"unverifiable\" branch to run silently, but the environment substituting a *different artifact\nentirely* for the one requested. Whether this counts as the second occurrence that promotes\n`sandbox-degraded-review-blind-spot` to its own topic is a judgment call for whoever next reviews that\nbar — the *shape* (environment quietly invalidates a gate result without erroring) matches; the\n*mechanism* (artifact substitution vs. capability block) doesn't.\n\n## Fix (harness) — applied\n\n`tests/lifecycle/harness.py` now gates on plugin identity before trusting results\n(merged to `lore-framework-dev` `main` 2026-07-27: A7 + follow-up hotfixes):\n\n1. **`verify_plugin_identity`** — once per process, asks the engine to report\n   `FRAMEWORK-ROOT` / `PLUGIN-VERSION` for the plugin that actually supplies lr skills, and\n   asserts against `LR_FRAMEWORK_DIR`.\n2. **VERSION form normalization** — accept bare `VERSION` (`30`) and plugin-manifest\n   `1.30.0` as the same identity (`normalize_framework_version`). Live Claude probes reported\n   the manifest form.\n3. **Codex deterministic preflight** — because Codex has no `--plugin-dir`, check that an\n   enabled `lr@lore-framework` marketplace `source` realpath-equals `LR_FRAMEWORK_DIR` and\n   that the plugin cache has a matching VERSION. After that preflight, the engine probe uses\n   **VERSION-only** (`require_root=False`): Codex always installs into a cache copy, so\n   `FRAMEWORK-ROOT` never equals the worktree realpath even when correct.\n4. **`run_matrix.py`** — runs the check once per engine and **skips that engine's modules** on\n   failure (exit 2), so a wrong install cannot produce a false green across 7 modules.\n5. **`run_engine`** — lazy same check on direct module invocation (`python3 tests/lifecycle/test_*.py`).\n\nOpt-out: `LR_SKIP_PLUGIN_IDENTITY=1` (debug only). Unit coverage:\n`tests/test_lifecycle_plugin_identity.py`.\n\n## A7 shipped with two holes — both closed 2026-07-28\n\nA7 passed on Cursor on 2026-07-27 and the suite still ran against v30. Reading the stored logs\nfound **two structural holes in `harness.py`**, plus a third defect in the evidence class of the\nCursor arm:\n\n1. **Per-run `framework_dir` overrides were never checked.** The docstring assumed overrides were\n   \"absolute-path copies of the same tree.\" `test_08` is the only scenario handed a *different*\n   tree — so the one test that needed verification was precisely the one skipping it.\n2. **The check was cached per process** (`_IDENTITY_CHECKED_FOR`). That proves identity at probe\n   time only, not that it still held for later runs in the same process.\n3. **The Cursor arm was a model self-report, not a check.** It asked the engine which plugin root\n   supplied its `lr` skills — unanswerable from inside a model. It reported\n   `PLUGIN-IDENTITY-OK 31 <worktree>` truthfully (it read `--plugin-dir`'s tree) while a different\n   tree served the skills. Codex was never exposed to this because\n   `check_codex_plugin_sources()` was filesystem-deterministic from the start; the asymmetry was\n   invisible because both engines \"had A7 coverage.\" See `a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md`.\n\n**Fixes applied** (`lore-framework-dev` main, `03067c1` / `848d3fc`):\n\n- Per-run `framework_dir` overrides are now verified, not assumed.\n- The per-process cache is replaced by a verdict inherited through an `engine|realpath|VERSION`\n  token, while each child subprocess still runs the deterministic filesystem check itself. This\n  also removed the `test_takeover` 420s timeout, which was `run_matrix` probing once per engine and\n  then every module subprocess re-probing.\n- **`check_cursor_plugin_sources()`** — walks `~/.cursor/plugins/{local,marketplaces,cache}` and\n  rejects any tree whose `VERSION` differs from `LR_FRAMEWORK_DIR`. Filesystem only, no engine\n  call. Against the real machine state it named both stale v30 trees immediately.\n\n**These fixes are themselves ungated** — no lifecycle run has exercised them. See\n`v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md` § ungated commit ledger.\n\n**Cursor operational companion:** disabling/repointing alone is incomplete — Cursor's cloud\nmarketplace install rehydrates the cache from GitHub over `--plugin-dir`, and does so within ~25\nseconds of a move-aside. See `cursor-cloud-plugin-rehydrates-over-plugin-dir.md`.\n\n## Concrete instance this bit\n\nThe 2026-07-27 *first* lifecycle run against the parked v31 branch (`v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md`):\nClaude/haiku produced the only trustworthy result (6/7 green, one flaky scenario root-caused and fixed\n— see `macos-var-symlink-realpath-ambiguity.md`); Codex and Cursor's results from that same run were\n**invalid, not red**.\n\nThe second run, after A7 + engine repoint, was only *partly* rescued: Codex's shard became valid,\nbut **Cursor's shard was still uninterpretable** — holes 1–3 above let it run on v30 again. That was\nfound only on 2026-07-28 by reading the stored logs. See\n`v31-lifecycle-rerun-partial-green-2026-07-27.md` for the corrected triage; do not trust the\noriginal six-item failure list from that run.\n\n## See Also\n\n- `lifecycle-testing-harness.md` — the harness this gap lives in.\n- `a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md` — the principle the Cursor arm violated; the general\n  rule this instance produced.\n- `cursor-cloud-plugin-rehydrates-over-plugin-dir.md` — Cursor-specific rehydration over `--plugin-dir`.\n- `transcript-vs-final-message-assertions.md` — the sibling harness-evidence defect found in the\n  same 2026-07-28 log review.\n- `v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md` — the concrete run this invalidated, plus the valid re-run.\n- `lore-beings-mvp-takeover-review.md` — the sibling sandboxed-review blind spot (blocked capability,\n  not artifact substitution).\n- `post-convergence-edits-need-their-own-gate.md` — the adjacent framing that a gate result belongs to\n  a specific artifact state; this is a sharper case where the state was never the intended one at all.\n- `macos-documents-permission-loss-mid-session.md` — another \"environment makes a run uninterpretable\"\n  case, different mechanism (permission loss vs. wrong artifact resolved).\n"
  }
]

Each lore agent lives in a git-backed markdown repository with a role, compact working context, and a knowledge graph of decisions, domain facts, operational runbooks, and session learnings. The framework provides skills to boot agents, recall their lore, reflect and merge new knowledge at session end, manage agent repos, initialize workspaces, and move sessions across Claude Code, Codex, and Cursor.\",\n    \"developerName\": \"Yaroslav Panasyuk\",\n    \"category\": \"Productivity\",\n    \"capabilities\": [\n      \"Read\",\n      \"Write\"\n    ],\n    \"websiteURL\": \"https://github.com/zroslaw/lore-framework\",\n    \"logo\": \"./assets/logo-512.png\",\n    \"composerIcon\": \"./assets/logo-256.png\"\n  }\n}\n--- release-notes/31.md\n# Release Notes -- Version 31\n\n## Summary\n\nVersion 31 adds **`scripts/lr-core`** — a deterministic substrate for the mechanical steps that boot,\nattach, consult, recall and pull-lore used to execute as prose, one tool call per step. Booting an\nagent now runs a single `preflight` command that discovers the agent, auto-pulls its repo, compares\nversion stamps, and detects an Agent-Teams spawn, then hands back one JSON report. Repeated pulls of\nthe same repo within a short window are skipped by a TTL cache, which is felt most in fan-outs\n(merge, consult, attach) that used to re-pull a repo pulled moments earlier.\n\nIt also introduces the **Script Fallback Contract** (`docs/conventions.md`): a shipped script must\nnever become a single point of failure. `scripts/lr-core` is a **literate accelerator**: its\nprocedure lives in its own instructional comments, not in a separate prose doc, so there is one\nartifact instead of two that can silently drift apart. Where a script *is* the specification\ninstead (no accelerator relationship at all), the agent reports the failure and stops that\noperation rather than improvising a substitute. Nothing was deleted — the hand-executable steps\nmoved from `docs/*.md` files into the functions they describe, and each doc's *restated procedure*\nbecame a short pointer into them.\n\nThat is not the same as the docs getting shorter overall, and most of them didn't: `auto-pull.md`\n(96→85) and `trilens-loop.md` (325→80) shrank, but `agent-boot.md` (103→165), `conventions.md`\n(325→411), `version-check.md` (162→175), `attach.md` (127→143), `lore-search.md` (103→120),\n`pull-lore.md` (102→114) and `being.md` (226→235) all grew. The growth is new material this\nrelease added — a Manual Boot Procedure fallback path, the § Pull Freshness section, the Script\nFallback Contract itself, and new version-skew cases — not the procedures that moved into the\nscript.\n\n## Clear Plugin Cache\n\nThis release **adds** a script (`scripts/lr-core`) and **modifies** SKILL-referenced docs\n(`docs/agent-boot.md`, `docs/attach.md`, `docs/consult.md`, `docs/lore-search.md`,\n`docs/pull-lore.md`, `docs/process-merge.md`, `docs/auto-pull.md`, `docs/conventions.md`,\n`docs/being.md`, `docs/takeover.md`, `docs/wait.md`, `docs/workspace-pull.md`,\n`docs/spawn-teammate.md`, `docs/teammate-conventions.md`, `docs/version-check.md`,\n`docs/trilens-loop.md`). Refresh\nbefore expecting the new boot path to run from an installed plugin copy — a stale cache will keep\nexecuting the old prose procedure, which still works but is slower.\n\nRepo migration required: **no** — nothing writes into agent repos; existing repos auto-stamp to 31\non next boot.\n\nAfter upgrading:\n\n1. **Claude Code:** `rm -rf ~/.claude/plugins/cache/lore-framework/` (targeted), or\n   `rm -rf ~/.claude/plugins/cache/` (broader fallback). Exit the current session and start a new one.\n2. **Cursor:** run `bash /path/to/lore-framework/scripts/cursor-refresh-plugin`, then start a fresh\n   `cursor-agent --plugin-dir /path/to/lore-framework` session.\n3. **Codex:** run `bash /path/to/lore-framework/scripts/codex-refresh-plugin`, then start a fresh\n   `codex` session.\n\n## What's New\n\n### `scripts/lr-core` — deterministic substrate\n\nOne Python 3 file, standard library only, no `pip install`. Three subcommands:\n\n| Subcommand | What it does |\n|---|---|\n| `discover` | Enumerate lore agent repos and their agents in a workspace. |\n| `preflight` | Resolve an agent, auto-pull its repo (TTL-cached), compare versions, detect a teammate spawn. |\n| `scan` | Manifest of an agent's lore topics with titles, git dates, and staleness flags. |\n\nEvery subcommand prints one JSON object — `{\"ok\", \"data\", \"warnings\", \"errors\"}` — and follows a\nuniform exit-code contract: **0** means the script ran to completion (degraded conditions such as a\nfailed pull are reported *inside* `data`, because they are results, not failures), **2** means it\ncould not complete and the caller must fall back to prose.\n\nBoot's happy path is now: run preflight → act on the report → read `role.md` and `lore-context.md` →\nconfirm. The script deliberately does **not** inline those two files: interpreting them is the\nagent's job, not the script's.\n\n### Pull freshness (TTL cache)\n\nEach successful auto-pull stamps its time inside the repo's git directory (`.git/lr-last-pull` — not\ncommitted, no `.gitignore` change). A subsequent pull within the window (default 600s) is reported\n`fresh` and skipped. `--fresh` bypasses it; `/lr:pull-lore` and the pre-merge pull both pass it,\nbecause an explicit refresh should always hit the network.\n\n### `scan` brings age-awareness to recall\n\n`docs/lore-search.md` now generates a topic manifest before dispatching the search subagent, so the\nsubagent picks what to read by *title* rather than guessing from filenames — and sees how old each\ntopic is. Findings from topics past the staleness threshold should be flagged as needing\nverification. Age is a caution flag, not a disqualifier.\n\n### Script Fallback Contract\n\nNew section in `docs/conventions.md`. When a doc delegates to a script and the script fails to\ncomplete, the engine must (1) tell the user in one line which script failed, (2) read the relevant\nfunction's comments in the script and execute those steps by hand to the same end state, and (3) diagnose briefly without stalling —\nnever abort the surrounding flow. A script that exits 0 while *reporting* a problem has succeeded;\nthat is data, not a takeover trigger.\n\nThe contract distinguishes two kinds of script. An **accelerator** is **literate**: its procedure\nlives in its own instructional comments, so the agent can take over by hand by reading the very\nfunction that failed — `scripts/lr-core` is the reference case. (There is no companion prose copy;\nthat is the point — see § Literate Accelerator below.) An\n**implementation** script *is* the specification (`scripts/workspace-pull`, `scripts/session-takeover`,\n`scripts/sync-cursor-skills`, `scripts/lr-emit` + `scripts/wait-server.py`, `scripts/lrb.py`); there\nis nothing to take over, so the agent reports the failure and stops that operation rather than\nimprovising an undocumented substitute. `scripts/lrb.py` (the Being Keeper) is the strictest case of\nthat rule: it enforces budget caps and kill-switches, and a model standing in for it would be exactly\nthe prompt-theater the Lore Beings design forbids.\n\n## What Changed\n\n- `scripts/lr-core` — every `cmd_*` function and the helpers it calls (`pull_repo`,\n  `compare_versions`, `detect_teammate`, `_resolve_agent`) now carry numbered-step docstrings\n  written as freestanding instructions, not code annotations. These comments **are** the\n  normative procedure (see § Literate Accelerator below); no behavioral change.\n- `docs/agent-boot.md` — happy path delegates to `lr-core preflight`; § Manual Boot Procedure no\n  longer restates the steps in prose — it points at `cmd_preflight`'s docstring and the three\n  helper functions it names.\n- `docs/attach.md` — guest discovery, pull, and version comparison collapse into one preflight\n  call; the failure-path pointer names `_resolve_agent`/`pull_repo`/`compare_versions` instead of\n  restating their steps.\n- `docs/consult.md` — host-side existence check uses `lr-core discover` to fail fast on a typo;\n  failure path points at `cmd_discover`'s docstring.\n- `docs/pull-lore.md`, `docs/process-merge.md` — auto-pull sites call preflight with `--fresh`;\ndiff --git a/.gitignore b/.gitignore\nindex 0cf8a2f..26817c8 100644\n--- a/.gitignore\n+++ b/.gitignore\n@@ -4,3 +4,6 @@\n # Python bytecode from bundled scripts\n __pycache__/\n *.pyc\n+\n+# macOS\n+.DS_Store\n"
  }

See `release-notes/30.md`, `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`, `workdir/draft-trilens-loop.md`.\n\n## Backfill discipline\n\nEvery finalization that lands a `VERSION` bump must add the new version's entry here — including kind, scope summary, and cache-affecting annotation. Backfill at the same finalization that ships the version is the principled timing — not \"we'll get to it later.\" Drift in the history list erodes the topic's value as a per-version classification index.\n\n**Scan the whole tail for gaps, not just the current version — the discipline is not self-healing (v21 lesson).** Appending only the current version's entry each ship silently carries forward any gap a prior slipped ship left. Landing v21 (2026-07-06), I found the history was missing **both** v20 and v21 — the v20 ship had slipped the backfill entirely, and appending only v21 would have left the v20 gap permanent. So at every ship, before adding the new entry, scan the tail between the last recorded version and the one shipping and backfill **all** missing entries in the same ship. Cheap check: `for v in <last+1>..<current>; do grep -c \"v$v \" versioning-release-types.md; done` — any `0` is a gap. This is a bounded mechanical sweep, so it belongs in the current ship (`feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md`). `/lr:check` has no check for this yet — a candidate: assert a history entry exists for every `release-notes/<N>.md` / `migrations/<N>.md` (see `consistency-checks.md`).\n\nThis discipline composes with the deferral-discipline rule (`feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md`): the backfill is a bounded mechanical task that fits in the current ship's scope. It also composes with the cache-clear-footer convention: the same finalization should both (a) ensure the release-notes has the footer if applicable, and (b) record the cache-affecting status in this history list.\n\nThe role.md responsibilities call this out explicitly — see `role.md` § Lore-Curation Disciplines.\n\n**\"Commit pending\" can accumulate across versions — verify `git HEAD` at session start.** When lore\nsays a version is \"built, commit pending,\" don't assume the last-described version is the committed\none. At the start of the v18/v19 ship, the public repo `HEAD` was still at v17 — *both* v18 (lr-wait,\nrecorded as \"commit pending\") and the whole port were uncommitted in one working tree, so shipping\nrequired untangling and committing in order (v18 first, then v19), not a single push. Ground the\ncurrent state in `git log`/`git HEAD`, not in the most recent lore entry (a `verify-before-acting`\ninstance — `verify-before-acting-on-suspected-bugs.md`).\n\n## In-band BETA refinement (post-v10 observation)\n\nWhen a BETA feature needs refinement after its initial release, the version-bump ceremony is **not** required. Pattern:\n\n1. **Edit the procedure doc** (`docs/<feature>.md`) — source of truth for current behavior.\n2. **Leave the release notes alone** (`release-notes/<N>.md`) — they're a historical record, frozen at the version they describe. A verbatim citation that goes stale post-refinement is acceptable.\n3. **No `VERSION` bump** — the BETA caveat (\"internal procedure may evolve based on real-world usage\") in the release notes is the contract that licenses this.\n4. **Update lore at finalization** — design-decisions topics for the feature reflect the *current* state, not the originally-shipped state.\n## main...origin/main\n M lore-repo.md\ndiff --git a/lore-repo.md b/lore-repo.md\nindex bd46d75..e9b9fc2 100644\n--- a/lore-repo.md\n+++ b/lore-repo.md\n@@ -1,6 +1,6 @@\n ---\n description: Lore agents dedicated to lore framework development\n-version: \"30\"\n+version: \"31\"\n repos:\n   - git@github.com:zroslaw/lore-framework.git\n ---\n"

Merge output is valuable on its own; don't hold it hostage to the summary.\n- **Any merge subagent failed** — do not commit in that repo. If one repo's push succeeds and another's fails (network, auth, conflicts), the public record is momentarily asymmetric — e.g., the host summary is visible while a guest summary isn't yet. The local commits are already made; the user can retry the failed repo's push manually. Do not roll back the successful repos to \"restore symmetry\" — that would discard work.\n\n## Invariants\n\n- **One commit per touched repo.** Reflect, merge, and summarize output land in a single commit per repo — not split across phases.\n- **Fully automated.** Finalize runs end-to-end without approval prompts.
